## Assistant

I’ve now learned the **full Baku Qualifying validation report** as the latest RaceVision evidence base. This is a very significant result, and it changes the confidence level of the live telemetry architecture substantially.

## My overall view

I would classify the Qualifying result as:

> **The live telemetry source question is essentially solved.**

The test did not merely show that Official F1 JWT can produce `Position.z` and `CarData.z`. It demonstrated that **Official F1 and OpenF1 are carrying the same underlying telemetry samples**, with OpenF1 arriving substantially later and suffering repeated connection gaps. The report found 179,973 location samples and 175,566 car-data samples matching Official at the exact source timestamp with **0.00 m / 0-unit difference**. fileciteturn1file0L11-L18 fileciteturn1file0L149-L163

That is much stronger evidence than we had after FP1/FP2 or even P3.

---

# 1. The biggest discovery: OpenF1 is a delayed re-publication

This is the most important finding in the entire report.

The test established:

```text
Official F1
Position.z / CarData.z
        │
        ▼
   same sample
        │
        ├──────────────► Official delivery
        │
        └──────────────► OpenF1 delivery
```

rather than:

```text
Official = one measurement
OpenF1   = independent measurement
```

The report found exact timestamp matches with **zero positional difference and zero telemetry-value difference** for the matched samples. fileciteturn1file0L11-L18

That means OpenF1 is extremely valuable to RaceVision, but its role should now be understood as:

**fallback / redundancy / independent transport path**

rather than:

**independent telemetry truth.**

This is a very important distinction for your future architecture.

---

# 2. Official F1 is now clearly the primary source

The evidence is overwhelming within this test:

| Attribute | Official F1 JWT | OpenF1 |
|---|---:|---:|
| Position | Available | Same underlying samples |
| Car telemetry | Available | Same underlying samples |
| Timing | Authoritative | Secondary |
| Position latency | ~2.2 s p50 | ~14–15 s p50 |
| Disconnects | 0 during Q1–Q3 | 24 |
| Data gaps | 0 gaps >2 s | Large holes |
| Archive match | 100% | N/A |
| Q-phase continuity | Continuous | Missing data |
| Authentication | F1 TV subscription | OpenF1 OAuth |

The official feed had **zero disconnects in Q1–Q3**, while OpenF1 had 24 disconnects and 13,051 driver-seconds of missing location. fileciteturn1file0L11-L18

So I agree completely with the report's conclusion:

> **Official JWT should become RaceVision's primary live telemetry source.**

And OpenF1 should remain.

---

# 3. The archive result is extremely important

This may be the second most valuable finding.

The report says:

**18,433 Position samples and 17,837 CarData samples were identical to the official archive at the same timestamps.**

That means:

```text
LIVE JWT
   ↓
RaceVision capture
   ↓
Official archive
```

has effectively been validated.

This is a very strong confirmation that the live decoder is not somehow corrupting or transforming the data incorrectly. fileciteturn1file0L231-L240

That gives you a very useful future architecture:

```text
LIVE
Official SignalR + JWT
       ↓
Canonical telemetry
       ↓
Database

HISTORICAL
Official Archive
       ↓
same decoder
       ↓
Canonical telemetry
       ↓
Database / Replay
```

That is exactly what you want for RaceVision's **live + replay** vision.

---

# 4. Your canonical vehicle state is now proven feasible

The report explicitly establishes that for every Position sample:

**CarData exists within ±240 ms**, while TimingData provides pit/position information. fileciteturn1file0L20-L21

So this isn't theoretical anymore.

RaceVision can construct:

```text
VehicleState
├── driver
├── team
├── x
├── y
├── z
├── speed
├── rpm
├── gear
├── throttle
├── brake
├── lap
├── sector
├── position
├── gap
├── tyre
├── pit state
└── source timestamp
```

from the Official feed alone.

That is a major milestone.

---

# 5. There is one critical correction to make in the vehicle-state logic

The report discovered:

> `Status = "OnTrack"` for all cars, including cars sitting in the garage.

This is important. fileciteturn1file0L82-L83

Therefore RaceVision **must not use Position.z `Status` as the vehicle visibility/physics state**.

The correct conceptual logic is more like:

```text
TimingData InPit
        +
speed
        +
displacement
        +
position history
```

to determine whether the car should be:

```text
MOVING
STATIONARY
PIT
GARAGE
HIDDEN
```

The report already observed the long Antonelli stationary episode where Official `Status` remained `OnTrack`. fileciteturn1file0L217-L218

This should become a decoder/state-normalisation requirement.

---

# 6. The `104` problem is now quantified

This isn't a minor issue anymore.

The report found:

**150,132 throttle = 104**  
**150,208 brake = 104**  
**150,127 both = 104**

That is approximately **38%** of CarData samples. fileciteturn1file0L85-L99

Therefore:

```text
104 ≠ 104%
```

RaceVision should canonicalise this to something like:

```text
104 → null/unavailable
```

or an explicit quality state.

Similarly, the **314 impossible gear values** need filtering/holding. fileciteturn1file0L85-L99

I would put this in the **decoder/data-normalisation layer**, not in Unity and not in Analytics.

That prevents every downstream subsystem having to remember the same rules.

---

# 7. DRS is currently an explicit gap

This is worth recording clearly.

P3 suggested DRS might be present through channel 45.

Qualifying established:

> **DRS channel 45 absent — 0 samples.**

fileciteturn1file0L85-L99

So RaceVision should **not attempt to infer DRS as a canonical telemetry field from this feed yet**.

For now:

```text
DRS = unavailable
```

rather than trying to manufacture a value.

That becomes especially important for future analytics.

---

# 8. The interpolation result is excellent news

This is one of the most positive findings for Phase 15.

The report measured:

**Linear interpolation error:**

- p50 = **1.18 m**
- p95 = **7.22 m**
- p99 = **10.3 m**

Whereas:

**Constant-velocity extrapolation error:**

- p50 = **2.33 m**
- p95 = **16.85 m**
- p99 = **27.4 m**

fileciteturn1file0L219-L230

This strongly supports the architecture you've already built:

> **Prefer interpolation over extrapolation whenever real samples are available.**

And it suggests something important for RaceVision:

### Phase 15 was fundamentally the right architectural approach.

We don't need to rip out the prediction system.

Instead:

```text
Official telemetry
       ↓
buffer
       ↓
interpolate where samples exist
       ↓
predict only across unavoidable gaps
```

rather than:

```text
predict everything
```

That distinction should remain.

---

# 9. I would not chase 10 Hz blindly anymore

Earlier we discussed approximately 10 Hz canonical persistence.

The Qualifying data gives us a more nuanced answer.

Official Position.z is effectively around:

**3.8–4.2 Hz per active car**

while samples are batched. fileciteturn1file0L82-L84

So I would **not manufacture 10 Hz telemetry simply for the database**.

Instead:

### Store the actual canonical source samples

and separately:

### Render at 60/120 FPS

using interpolation.

That is much cleaner.

A 10 Hz database rate can still be useful for some derived-state/snapshot workloads, but it shouldn't imply that the source itself is 10 Hz.

---

# 10. The live presentation delay should now be treated as a first-class RaceVision concept

The Official Position.z p50 was around:

**2.16–2.23 seconds**

and the report estimates a smooth presentation target around **~3 seconds behind wall clock**, allowing for the source cadence/buffer. fileciteturn1file0L128-L142

I would therefore formalise:

```text
Wall Clock
    │
    │ ~3 sec
    ▼
RaceVision Presentation Clock
    │
    ▼
Canonical telemetry
```

This needs to be visible to the user as:

**Feed delay: ~X.X s**

rather than pretending the broadcast twin is literally showing the physical circuit at that exact instant.

That fits your Phase 15 architecture very well.

---

# 11. The OpenF1 result is actually better for RaceVision than I initially expected

Ironically, proving that OpenF1 carries the same samples makes it **more** useful.

Because RaceVision can now do:

```text
Official healthy
      ↓
Official primary
```

and if it fails:

```text
Official unavailable
      ↓
OpenF1 delayed copy
      ↓
timestamp-based recovery
```

The report says exact sample matching was proven, so the handover can be based on **source timestamp**, rather than attempting to reconcile two different physical coordinate systems. fileciteturn1file0L149-L163

That's excellent.

---

# 12. The OpenF1 connection problem is now understood

The report found:

**24 disconnects**

with a sawtooth latency pattern:

```text
4–7 s
   ↓
10 s
   ↓
15 s
   ↓
20+ s
   ↓
disconnect
   ↓
reconnect
   ↓
4–7 s
```

The report also explicitly says the capture system's own write path was benchmarked at roughly **1,680 msg/s**, versus about **200 msg/s required**, so the capture hub was not proven to be the cause. fileciteturn1file0L128-L147

That's useful because the OpenF1 issue shouldn't be misclassified as simply:

> "our Python client is too slow."

The exact cause still needs to remain documented as unresolved unless further evidence proves it.

But from RaceVision's perspective, that's not actually a blocker:

**OpenF1 is the fallback, not the primary.**

---

# 13. The OpenF1 duplicate problem has been properly identified

The 50% duplication was caused by:

```text
v1/location
+
v1/#
```

subscribing to the same messages.

The report says that is now fixed for the race. fileciteturn1file0L7-L7

That's exactly the sort of prototype issue we wanted to catch before production.

The worker queue architecture is also a good improvement:

```text
MQTT callback
      ↓
fast enqueue
      ↓
worker
      ↓
parse/store
```

rather than doing heavy work directly inside the MQTT callback.

The self-test of 5,000 messages with 0 lost and ordered processing is useful evidence, although I'd still distinguish that from real-race production validation.

---

# 14. I would now stop expanding the telemetry-source investigation

This is perhaps my strongest recommendation.

We have now tested:

---

# 15. What I would build next

Now I would move into the actual RaceVision production integration.

### Step 1 — Official JWT lifecycle

Make:

```text
OfficialF1SignalRProvider
```

responsible for:

- secure token loading
- expiry detection
- refresh/re-authentication
- connection recovery
- subscription restoration

Do not put any of this in Unity.

---

### Step 2 — Official `.z` production decoding

Move the proven test logic into the actual provider boundary:

```text
SignalR
 ↓
PositionZDecoder
 ↓
CarDataZDecoder
```

Your report already shows the decoder is capable of decoding the live data correctly and reproducing the official archive. fileciteturn1file0L231-L240

---

### Step 3 — Canonicalisation

Create the clean RaceVision state:

```text
Official raw
    ↓
normalise
    ↓
canonical VehicleState
```

with:

```text
104 → unavailable
gear > 8 → invalid/hold-last
0,0,0 → not live / hidden candidate
Status → DO NOT use for garage detection
```

---

### Step 4 — Timestamp/provenance

Every state should retain:

```text
source
source_timestamp
received_timestamp
latency
quality
```

This becomes extremely valuable for debugging and replay.

---

### Step 5 — Provider arbitration

Eventually:

```text
Official healthy
      ↓
Official


Official unavailable
      ↓
OpenF1


Official recovers
      ↓
stabilise
      ↓
Official
```

But do this **after** the basic Official integration is working.

---

# 16. I would not yet change Unity

This is important.

Unity should continue to consume the existing canonical/synchronisation interfaces.

Do not make Unity understand:

```text
Position.z
CarData.z
OpenF1
JWT
SignalR
```

Unity should know:

```text
VehicleState
```

only.

That preserves the architecture you've spent Phases 10–15 building.

---

# 17. I would not change Phase 15 fundamentally

The Qualifying evidence actually validates the strategy.

The measured result says:

```text
REAL SAMPLE
   ↓
INTERPOLATE
   ↓
~1.18 m typical error
```

is significantly better than:

```text
REAL SAMPLE
   ↓
EXTRAPOLATE
   ↓
~2.33 m typical error
```

with the difference becoming much larger at the tail. fileciteturn1file0L219-L230

So I would refine Phase 15 rather than replace it.

---

# 18. The report gives you a very good Phase 16 foundation

You can now derive:

### Driver analytics

From:

```text
speed
rpm
gear
throttle
brake
position
lap timing
sector timing
tyres
```

you can calculate things such as:

```text
braking points
braking duration
throttle pickup
corner exit speed
straight-line speed
gear behaviour
lap delta
sector delta
stint performance
```

### Battle intelligence

You have:

```text
position
gap
speed
location
lap
timing
```

which are enough to begin detecting:

```text
approach
closing
attack window
under/overcut context
position change
```

### Incident intelligence

You have:

```text
race control
track status
position
speed
stationary state
timing
```

which gives you a solid basis for detecting incidents and associating them with official messages.

So the telemetry work is now feeding directly into Phase 16.

---

# 19. One thing I would add before Phase 16 analytics

I'd create a **Telemetry Quality Layer**.

Something like:

```text
VehicleTelemetry
       ↓
Quality / Normalisation
       ↓
CanonicalVehicleState
       ↓
Prediction / Sync
       ↓
Analytics
```

with quality flags such as:

```text
VALID
INTERPOLATED
PREDICTED
STALE
UNAVAILABLE
FALLBACK_OPENF1
```

This would be extremely useful.

For example:

```text
speed = 285
source = OFFICIAL
quality = VALID
```

versus:

```text
speed = 285
source = OPENF1
quality = FALLBACK
```

versus:

```text
speed = 285
source = PREDICTION
quality = PREDICTED
```

Analytics can then decide whether a measurement is suitable for a particular calculation.

---

# 20. There is one architectural point I would be especially careful about

The report says:

> "OpenF1 is not an independent measurement."

I agree for the **matched position/car-data samples demonstrated in Qualifying**. But I would **not generalise that statement to every OpenF1 endpoint or every future dataset without testing**.

For RaceVision purposes, the safe engineering rule is:

> **Treat OpenF1 position and car_data as a secondary delivery path for the same underlying telemetry where equivalence has been demonstrated.**

That's precise and defensible.

For OpenF1-specific endpoints such as laps, stints, pit or other data, treat their semantics independently and continue field-level validation.

---

# 21. What I think you have achieved

The project has moved through three stages:

```text
STAGE 1
Can we get F1 live data?
        ↓
YES


STAGE 2
Can we get usable 3D car motion?
        ↓
YES — OpenF1


STAGE 3
Can we get Official authenticated motion?
        ↓
YES — F1 TV JWT


STAGE 4
Is Official better and reliable enough?
        ↓
Qualifying evidence strongly supports YES


STAGE 5
Can we construct a canonical RaceVision vehicle state?
        ↓
YES


STAGE 6
Integrate into RaceVision production architecture
        ↓
NEXT
```

That is a very substantial milestone.

---

## My recommended next RaceVision sequence

I would now stop doing experimental provider discovery and proceed in this order:

**1. Official F1 JWT lifecycle + provider integration**

**2. Production `Position.z` / `CarData.z` decoding + normalisation**

**3. Canonical `VehicleState` integration**

**4. Official-primary / OpenF1-fallback arbitration**

**5. Synchronisation/interpolation integration with the existing Phase 15 runtime**

**6. Replay/archive validation using the now-proven Official archive**

**7. Race-session validation**

**8. Then Phase 16 analytics/commentary**

That sequence keeps your existing architecture intact while incorporating everything Baku has now taught us.

The Qualifying report is strong enough that I would consider the **Official F1 JWT telemetry path experimentally validated for RaceVision's primary live telemetry role**, with the normal caveat that Saturday's Race is the final high-load real-world validation before calling the runtime production-ready. fileciteturn1file0L11-L18

And one particularly valuable result should not be lost: **live Official telemetry matching the Official post-session archive exactly** gives RaceVision a credible path toward one consistent telemetry model for both **live viewing and historical replay**. fileciteturn1file0L231-L240

## Assistant

Understood. I’ve incorporated this **final Qualifying report and Race-readiness state** into my working understanding of RaceVision.

This is now the strongest evidence we have so far:

**Official F1 + F1 TV JWT is the primary live telemetry source.**

The most decisive finding is that the Qualifying OpenF1 `location` and `car_data` samples matched the Official JWT samples at the same source timestamps with **0.00 m / zero channel differences**, while OpenF1 arrived roughly **12 seconds later** and lost about **22% of samples** through repeated disconnects.

That means the architecture is now much clearer:

```text
Official F1 + JWT
        │
        ├── Position.z
        ├── CarData.z
        ├── TimingData
        ├── Tyres
        ├── Race Control
        ├── Weather
        └── Session State
                │
                ▼
        RaceVision Canonical State
                │
        ├── Synchronisation
        ├── Prediction
        ├── Unity
        ├── Analytics
        └── Director


OpenF1 MQTT
        │
        ├── Same underlying motion/car samples
        ├── Delayed
        └── Sometimes incomplete
                │
                ▼
        FALLBACK / REDUNDANCY
```

### The other findings I’m carrying forward

The Official feed is effectively **loss-free relative to the F1 archive** for the captured Position and CarData data. That is a particularly valuable validation for RaceVision's live/replay architecture.

The Official stream provides enough information to construct a useful canonical vehicle state:

`X/Y/Z + speed + RPM + gear + throttle + brake + lap/timing + pit + tyre + position`

without depending on OpenF1.

The two important normalisation rules are now established:

`104` on throttle/brake = unavailable/sentinel, not 104%.

Gear values above 8 are bad data and need filtering/hold-last behaviour.

And the Position `Status` field **must not** be used to decide whether a car is actually running, because it remained `OnTrack` even for a stationary/garage car. Stopped/visibility logic therefore needs TimingData `InPit` plus speed/displacement/state history.

The interpolation evidence is also strong: interpolation was measured as substantially more accurate than constant-velocity extrapolation, supporting the existing Phase 15 design rather than replacing it.

### One thing I particularly like about the Race test preparation

The three experimental fixes are exactly the right things to capture before production integration:

**OpenF1 duplicate removal**  
**Official full-state restoration after reconnect**  
**True MQTT socket-arrival + queue-wait measurements**

That last one is especially useful because it should finally tell us whether the OpenF1 14–25 second latency is originating upstream or inside our capture pipeline.

And the benchmark result gives useful evidence that the hub itself was not obviously overloaded: approximately **1,680 msg/s capacity versus ~200 msg/s required**.

### Therefore, I would now treat the project as being at this point

```text
Provider research
       ✓

FP1 / FP2 dual-feed experiment
       ✓

FP3 JWT proof
       ✓

Qualifying full comparison
       ✓

Official vs archive validation
       ✓

Primary provider decision
       ✓

Production integration
       → NEXT

Race validation
       → TOMORROW

Phase 16 telemetry-driven analytics
       → AFTER CORE TELEMETRY INTEGRATION
```

So I would **not do another round of “which provider should we use?” research** after the Race unless something unexpected happens.

The next major engineering job is now to take the experimentally proven behaviour and integrate it into the **existing RaceVision provider → canonical telemetry → synchronisation → prediction → Unity architecture**, while preserving OpenF1 as fallback.

I’ve got the Qualifying results and the Race-ready state in context for your next RaceVision question.

## Assistant

Yes — I’ve reviewed and learned the **full Race Conditions validation report** you provided. This is now the strongest RaceVision telemetry evidence from the complete Baku weekend.

### What the Race proved

The critical result is that the **Official F1 JWT path remained reliable through an entire race**:

- Official `Position.z`: **5,773 race frames**, zero decode failures, 22 cars/frame.
- Official `CarData.z`: **479,314 samples**, zero decode failures.
- Official TimingData produced a coherent 22-car final classification.
- Official had **one brief reconnect**, restored a 29-topic snapshot, and returned to live data in under 0.5 s.
- OpenF1 had **two race disconnects**. fileciteturn3file0L15-L44

That means the conclusion we've been working toward is now supported by **practice, qualifying, and the full race**, not just a short test.

### The most important new race-only finding

The Official race-specific topics are now understood:

| Race capability | Official result |
|---|---|
| `LapCount` | **Present** |
| `DriverRaceInfo` | **Present** |
| `ChampionshipPrediction` | **Present** |
| `PitStop` | **Absent** |
| `PitStopSeries` | **Absent** |
| `OvertakeSeries` | **Absent** |
| `DriverScore` | **Absent** |
| `RcmSeries` | **Absent** |
| `SPFeed` | **Absent** |

`DriverRaceInfo` is particularly useful because it carries **Gap, Interval, Catching, OvertakeState, IsOut, PitStops and Position**. fileciteturn3file0L90-L131 fileciteturn3file0L164-L190

So RaceVision does **not** need to wait for Official `PitStop` or `OvertakeSeries` feeds that aren't actually being supplied.

### Pit stops are solved

This is another important architectural result.

The race produced **38 reconstructed pit stops**, using:

**TimingData `InPit` + CarData speed + PitLaneTimeCollection + CurrentTyres + OpenF1 `pit`**

and the report found that Official `PitLaneTimeCollection.Duration` agrees with OpenF1 `pit` where both are present. fileciteturn3file0L194-L241

So I would not design a RaceVision dependency on an Official `PitStop` topic.

Instead:

```text id="pitarch"
Timing / PitLane / Tyres
          +
Position / CarData
          +
OpenF1 pit as corroboration/fallback
          ↓
     Canonical PitStopEvent
```

### Overtakes are also workable

Official `OvertakeSeries` stayed silent, but OpenF1 produced **160 overtake records**, while Official TimingData provides the position changes needed to support reconstruction. fileciteturn3file0L245-L285

Therefore I'd make overtakes a **derived RaceVision event**, rather than a provider-specific event.

That is actually a better architecture:

```text
Position changes
+
Timing
+
Pit state
+
OpenF1 overtakes
        ↓
 RaceVision OvertakeEvent
```

with confidence/provenance.

### Gaps and intervals are excellent

This is another strong result.

Official TimingData provided `GapToLeader` and `IntervalToPositionAhead` throughout the race, while OpenF1 `intervals` matched the values closely. fileciteturn3file0L289-L429

That means the analytics/battle system can use **Official timing as the authority** without depending on OpenF1.

### Starting grid is solved too

Official `TimingAppData.GridPos` provided the starting grid, while OpenF1 `starting_grid` was silent. fileciteturn3file0L433-L462

So again:

**Official should own this field.**

### Final classification is solved

The complete race classification can be reconstructed from folded Official TimingData, including laps, pit counts, gaps, intervals and retirement/stopped state. OpenF1 `session_result` was silent. fileciteturn3file0L466-L493

That's important for the future replay/statistics system.

---

# One particularly important discovery for Phase 15

The race confirmed that we should **not treat every zero-speed interval as a generic failure**.

You now have enough information to distinguish:

```text
MOVING
STATIONARY
PIT
PIT_STATIONARY
FINISHED
RETIRED
HIDDEN
```

using telemetry plus Timing/DriverRaceInfo state rather than Position `Status`. fileciteturn3file0L580-L743

That's exactly the kind of state normalisation that belongs **before** prediction and Unity presentation.

---

# Safety Car behaviour is now proven

The Race included:

- **2 Safety Car periods**
- ~788 s
- ~373 s
- no VSC
- no red flag

fileciteturn3file0L864-L894

This is valuable because now the RaceVision prediction layer has a genuine real-race case for:

```text
NORMAL
   ↓
SAFETY CAR
   ↓
RESTART
   ↓
NORMAL
```

rather than just testing these states synthetically.

---

# The OpenF1 result is interesting

Qualifying made OpenF1 look dramatically worse.

The Race did something different:

**Official Position.z p50 ≈ 2.90 s**  
**OpenF1 location p50 ≈ 3.15 s**

So in this particular Race, OpenF1 was only about **0.25 s behind** Official, while still having two disconnects. fileciteturn3file0L19-L40

That tells me we should **not hard-code a fixed latency assumption** for OpenF1.

Instead RaceVision should dynamically measure:

```text
source_timestamp
receive_timestamp
```

and select the source based on health/quality, not a permanently assumed "Official = 2 seconds / OpenF1 = 15 seconds".

That's an important refinement.

---

# The archive validation is extremely strong

The report confirms:

**826,764 Position samples = archive match**  
**791,692 CarData samples = archive match**

with **100% equality on common samples**. fileciteturn3file0L15-L37

This is one of the strongest pieces of evidence for the entire RaceVision project because it connects:

```text
LIVE
Official SignalR + JWT
        ↓
RaceVision decoder
        ↓
Canonical data

             ↕

ARCHIVE
Official F1 static archive
```

So the same telemetry model can credibly support:

**Live + Replay + Historical Analysis**

which is one of the original RaceVision goals.

---

# I would now officially close the provider-discovery phase

Across the weekend:

```text id="weekendstatus"
FP1
  Official timing
  OpenF1 motion

FP2
  Official timing
  OpenF1 motion

FP3
  Official JWT motion confirmed

QUALIFYING
  Official = same telemetry
  OpenF1 = delayed/incomplete

RACE
  Official = complete primary telemetry
  OpenF1 = fallback/supplement
  Race-only channels investigated
  Archive equivalence proven
```

I don't think there is much value in doing another large-scale investigation of:

> "Which feed should RaceVision use?"

The evidence is now sufficient.

---

# The next step should be production integration

I agree with the report's proposed next steps:

1. **Filter `104` in `CarDataZDecoder`.**
2. **Handle invalid gear values defensively.**
3. **Implement stationary/retired detection without relying on Position `Status`.**
4. **Ensure `SubscribeCompletion` restores full Official state after reconnect.**
5. **Add Official-primary/OpenF1-fallback arbitration behind a feature flag.**

Those are now evidence-backed engineering tasks rather than speculative architecture. fileciteturn3file0L580-L743 fileciteturn3file0L848-L860

I would add one more:

**6. Make source/field provenance part of canonical telemetry.**

For example:

```text
position_source = OFFICIAL
car_data_source = OFFICIAL
timing_source = OFFICIAL
pit_source = DERIVED
overtake_source = DERIVED + OPENF1
```

with:

```text
source_timestamp
received_timestamp
latency_ms
quality
```

That will make the future fallback and analytics architecture considerably safer.

---

## My final RaceVision assessment

I now see the system as:

```text id="finalarch"
              OFFICIAL F1 + JWT
                     │
        ┌────────────┼────────────┐
        │            │            │
   Position.z    CarData.z    TimingData
        │            │            │
        └──────┬─────┴────────────┘
               │
        Canonical VehicleState
               │
        ┌──────┼──────────┐
        ▼      ▼          ▼
   Synchronize Prediction Analytics
        │      │          │
        └──────┼──────────┘
               ▼
             Unity
               │
            Director
               │
          Broadcast


        OPENF1 MQTT
             │
       fallback / supplement
       pits / overtakes / etc.
```

And the important thing is that **this architecture is now supported by actual FP1 → FP2 → FP3 → Qualifying → Race evidence**, including live-to-archive validation. fileciteturn3file0L15-L44

I would therefore move RaceVision from **telemetry investigation** into **production telemetry integration and hardening** next. The Race report is the point where I would consider the telemetry-provider research phase effectively complete.

1 — EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

Final high-level finding:

OFFICIAL F1 + F1 TV JWT is the PRIMARY live telemetry source for RaceVision.

OPENF1 remains:

- the option when F1 TV authentication is unavailable.

The Race confirmed the architecture under full-race conditions.

This is a public/live telemetry architecture, NOT full internal F1 team engineering telemetry.

4 — OFFICIAL F1 WITH F1 TV JWT
========================================================

Explain that FP3 was the first live proof.

FP3:
- Position.z = 4,559 frames
- CarData.z = 4,551 frames
- 0 decode failures
- all 22 cars represented per frame
- typical Official latency ~2.1–2.3 s p50

Qualifying:
- Position.z = 4,819 frames
- CarData.z = 4,777 frames
- 0 decode failures
- 22 cars per frame
- approximately 3.8 Hz per active car
- p50 approximately 2.17–2.23 s
- p95 approximately 2.64–2.73 s

Race:
- 5,773 Position.z frames in race window
- 479,314 CarData.z samples
- zero decode failures
- full-race continuity
- official Position latency approximately 2.90 s p50
- one reconnect during the race
- snapshot restored and live data resumed in less than 0.5 s

Explain that this transformed Official F1 from timing-only into the primary live car-motion source.

11 — POSITION / MOTION DATA
========================================================

Explain:

Official Position.z is a batched snapshot stream.

It does NOT mean one message per car.

Typical:
~4 snapshots / frame
~22 cars per frame
effective active-car sample cadence about 3.8–4.2 Hz in Qualifying/Race.

Qualifying:
per-car interval p50 about 240 ms
p95 about 420 ms.

Race:
Position frame interarrival about 1 second because updates are batched.

State that raw frame count must not be compared directly against OpenF1 per-car message count.

Explain coordinate units where measured:

Official Position.z values were measured at approximately 10 internal units per metre.

Do not overstate this as official published documentation unless clearly labelled as measured.

========================================================

Explain measured channels.

Document observed ranges and quality.

Known issues:

Throttle = 104
Brake = 104

These are unavailable/sentinel values, NOT percentages.

Qualifying had approximately:
150,132 throttle sentinel occurrences
150,208 brake sentinel occurrences

Race had:
54,603 throttle sentinel
54,749 brake sentinel

RPM = 0 can indicate non-running/stationary/no telemetry state.

Impossible gear values occurred in Qualifying:
314 values > 8

Race improved:
0 gear >8 in the final capture.

Speed >380 km/h:
0 observed.

DRS:
channel 45 absent in Qualifying and Race.

Explicit production rule:

104 → unavailable/null

Never treat 104 as 104%.

========================================================
37 — INTERPOLATION / PREDICTION
========================================================

Document validated findings.

Qualifying measured:

Linear midpoint interpolation:
p50 ~1.18 m error
p95 ~7.22 m
p99 ~10.3 m

Constant velocity extrapolation:
p50 ~2.33 m
p95 ~16.85 m
p99 ~27.4 m

Interpretation:

Interpolation between real samples was more accurate than future extrapolation in the tested data.

RaceVision should therefore:

real sample
↓
timestamped buffer
↓
interpolation
↓
prediction only when a real sample is unavailable
↓
render 60/120 FPS

Document that Phase 15 was NOT replaced by this result.

It was reinforced/refined.

### The key benefit of doing it this way

This will turn the Baku work from a collection of **separate investigation reports** into a permanent project knowledge base.

The README will show the progression:

**FP1/FP2 → Official timing + OpenF1 motion**

**FP3 → JWT unlocks Official Position.z/CarData.z**

**Qualifying → Official and OpenF1 proven to contain the same underlying samples, with major OpenF1 delivery losses**

**Race → full-race validation, pit reconstruction, race-only topics, Safety Car behaviour, archive equivalence**

That gives future Cursor agents a single place to understand why RaceVision uses **Official F1 + JWT as primary and OpenF1 as fallback/supplement**, rather than having to rediscover the reasoning from old chat logs. fileciteturn3file0L15-L44

The most important part is that the prompt tells Cursor to preserve the distinction between **what the F1 feeds actually provide** and **what RaceVision derives from them**. That will become especially valuable when you begin integrating the telemetry into the Phase 10–15 runtime and then Phase 16 analytics.

- **Phase 13 — OBS / Broadcast Egress — Accepted with limitations**

- **Phase 15 — Prediction & Latency Compensation — Accepted with limitations**
- **Phase 16 — Race Analytics & Commentary Intelligence — Accepted with environment limitations**
- **Phase 17 — Public API & External Integrations — complete**
- **Phase 18 — Production Hardening & Production Content — currently underway**

So the README is not just missing the Baku telemetry findings — **it describes an earlier version of the project**.

I would **not use the previous Cursor prompt unchanged**.

### Why this revised prompt is better for your repository

I checked the **actual README and current roadmap**, and the key issue is that we don't want Cursor to accidentally create a beautiful telemetry section while leaving the top of the README saying:

> “Application features … are intentionally not implemented yet.”

That would make the repository internally contradictory.

The README should now reflect **where RaceVision actually is**:

```text
Phases 10–17
     │
     ▼
Architecture / runtime established
     │
     ▼
Baku telemetry validation
     │
     ▼
Official JWT primary confirmed
     │
     ▼
Phase 18
Production hardening + production content
     │
     ▼
Production race-day system
```

And this is especially important because `docs/Telemetry.md` already contains the canonical architectural principles around provider → adapter/collector → validation/normalisation → PostgreSQL → WebSocket → Unity/replay, so the README should **summarise rather than compete with that document**.

I’d use the revised prompt above in Cursor. It is tailored to the **actual RaceVision repository you have today**, not the much older README state.

## Assistant

Absolutely. I now have a much more complete picture of RaceVision than just the original architecture. The important thing is that the project has moved from **“build the architecture”** into **“prove and harden the real race-day system.”**

I also re-read the current `main` branch documentation and provider structure. The repository has already absorbed a substantial amount of the Baku telemetry work, although there are still some documentation/status inconsistencies that should be cleaned up as part of the integration.

## 1. What RaceVision actually is now

RaceVision is no longer just a proposed F1 visualisation project.

It is now designed as a **single canonical motorsport runtime**:

```text
                 LIVE / HISTORICAL SOURCES
                          │
          ┌───────────────┼────────────────┐
          │               │                │
   Official F1       OpenF1 MQTT       Archives/
   SignalR + JWT        / REST          FastF1
          │               │                │
          └───────────────┼────────────────┘
                          ▼
              Provider Adapters
                          ▼
              Validation / Normalisation
                          ▼
               CANONICAL TELEMETRY
                          ▼
                ONE SESSION RUNTIME
                          │
          ┌───────────────┼──────────────────┐
          ▼               ▼                  ▼
       Replay        Prediction          Analytics
                                            │
                                            ▼
                                      Commentary
                          │
             ┌────────────┴────────────┐
             ▼                         ▼
          Unity                    Director
             │                         │
             └────────────┬────────────┘
                          ▼
                    OBS / Broadcast
                          │
              Twitch / Kick / YouTube
                          │
                      Public API
```

The most important architectural rule is:

> **There must never be a second competing runtime containing “live truth”.**

Live, replay, prediction, analytics, commentary, Unity, director and public API all consume the same underlying runtime state.

That principle has been preserved through Phases 10–17 and is explicitly retained as a Phase 18 invariant.

---

# 2. The completed phases

## Phases 0–9

These established the basic platform:

- repository structure
- configuration
- PostgreSQL
- ORM
- migrations
- repositories
- unit-of-work
- query objects
- protocols
- provider framework
- CI/tooling
- normalization and telemetry foundations

These are no longer the main architectural challenge.

---

# 3. Phase 10 — Session and Replay Runtime

Phase 10 was the major architectural turning point.

It established:

### Session model

A race weekend/session is a first-class runtime concept.

There are explicit concepts for:

- season
- meeting
- session
- track session
- drivers
- cars
- race events
- timeline
- snapshots

### Live and Replay convergence

This is critical.

RaceVision does **not** have:

```text
LiveRuntime
ReplayRuntime
```

as two unrelated engines.

Instead:

```text
              Session Runtime
             /              \
          LIVE             REPLAY
            \                /
             same timeline
             same snapshot
             same consumers
```

That is what makes future historical replay, analysis, commentary and deterministic testing possible.

### Delay buffer

The delay buffer is a **source-time runtime mechanism**, not an OBS streaming delay.

It exists to support the target race visualisation delay architecture.

Current default:

```text
Delay buffer = OFF
```

### Interpolation

Gap detection exists.

Interpolation exists as a controlled derived mechanism.

But:

```text
Authoritative telemetry
        ≠
Interpolated telemetry
```

and authoritative samples are never overwritten.

Interpolation is currently conservative and off by default.

### Snapshot

RaceVision has an immutable race state snapshot concept.

That becomes important because Unity, APIs, analytics and other consumers should consume stable state rather than rummaging around inside providers or database internals.

---

# 4. Phase 11 — Transport

Phase 11 established the external transport boundary.

The important architecture is:

```text
Client
  ↓
REST / WebSocket
  ↓
shared transport contracts
  ↓
existing runtime/controllers
  ↓
Phase 10 runtime
  ↓
RaceStateSnapshot / Events / Telemetry
```

Not:

```text
REST → database
REST → provider
REST → ORM
REST → internal runtime cache
```

That distinction is extremely important.

Phase 11 also established:

- REST runtime API
- WebSocket runtime gateway
- command transport
- snapshot streaming
- live telemetry streaming
- authentication
- authorization
- rate limiting
- transport hardening
- replay streaming
- outbound backpressure
- subscriptions
- connection management
- transport observability
- integration testing
- acceptance

Two roadmap items were intentionally superseded:

- 11.10 by 11.7.1
- 11.11 by 11.6

That should remain documented exactly that way.

---

# 5. Phase 12 — Unity

Unity is now defined as a **consumer**, not a source of truth.

The implemented architecture includes:

- WebSocket transport
- state synchronisation
- coordinate system
- vehicle runtime
- multi-car rendering
- vehicle transforms
- track scene runtime
- telemetry visualisation
- vehicle effects
- LIVE/REPLAY presentation mode
- performance budgets
- Unity integration tests

The architecture rule is:

```text
Backend owns state
Unity renders state
```

Unity must not invent authoritative car positions.

There are still environment limitations:

- Unity project openability needs completion
- `Packages/manifest.json` was missing in the earlier acceptance baseline
- actual Unity execution was not available during those acceptance tests
- production `.unity` scenes and `.prefab` content were still missing

That is now Phase 18 territory.

---

# 6. Phase 13 — OBS / Broadcast

The broadcast architecture exists.

There are state engines for:

- leaderboard
- timing/lap
- telemetry
- battle
- incidents
- scene management

There is an OBS adapter.

There are platform adapters.

But this was deliberately architected before doing real race-day validation.

Therefore:

```text
software integration ≠ proven live production
```

Remaining limitations include:

- live OBS validation
- real stream-platform validation
- production OBS scene collection
- actual broadcast graphics
- audio package
- VFX
- production visual content

---

# 7. Phase 14 — Camera Director

Phase 14 is one of the most misunderstood parts of the project.

It is **not** a camera renderer.

It is a **presentation decision system**.

The architecture now includes:

```text
Camera Registry
       ↓
Camera State
       ↓
Tracking State
       ↓
Control Intent
       ↓
Movement Plan
       ↓
Candidate Selection
       ↓
Priority
       ↓
Transition Plan
       ↓
Director Timeline
       ↓
OBS Translation
```

But the engines intentionally stop before executing real Unity cameras/OBS operations.

The central authority remains:

### 14.7 DirectorDecisionEngine

It chooses.

It does not execute.

That distinction must remain intact.

The same applies to:

- battle director
- incident director
- cockpit director
- replay director
- priority engine
- transition engine
- timeline engine
- OBS director engine
- camera analytics
- director performance

This is a very good architectural boundary because later Unity/OBS implementation can be replaced without rewriting the director policy.

---

# 8. Phase 15 — Prediction

Phase 15 established prediction as a **derived subsystem**.

This is extremely important for the telemetry system you have now validated.

Prediction is allowed to say:

> “I estimate where the vehicle should be.”

It is **not** allowed to say:

> “This estimated position is now the authoritative truth.”

So:

```text
Official telemetry
      ↓
Canonical observed state
      ↓
Prediction
      ↓
Derived predicted state
```

not:

```text
Prediction
   ↓
overwrite canonical telemetry
```

Phase 15 includes:

- prediction architecture
- state model
- short horizon
- 5-second prediction
- trajectory prediction
- smoothing
- error correction
- live-vs-predicted comparison
- confidence
- performance
- failure handling

The Phase 15 acceptance result was:

**ACCEPTED WITH LIMITATIONS**

with approximately:

- 172 Phase 15 tests
- 1,606 previous-phase regressions
- clean Ruff/Mypy
- roughly 82.9% prediction coverage

The main unresolved issue is not architecture.

It is validation against sustained real race telemetry.

---

# 9. Phase 16 — Analytics and Commentary

Phase 16 established:

- race analytics runtime
- driver performance analytics
- battle detection
- strategy analytics
- incident intelligence
- predictive race analytics
- commentary event generation
- AI commentary engine
- commentary context
- TTS
- commentary safety/rate control

Again, these are derived consumers.

They should never become a new telemetry authority.

The conceptual flow is:

```text
Canonical race state
        ↓
Analytics
        ↓
Events
        ↓
Commentary context
        ↓
Commentary generation
        ↓
TTS
```

not:

```text
LLM interprets raw telemetry
          ↓
becomes race state
```

That architectural distinction is now important because we actually have evidence for what the real feed does and does not contain.

---

# 10. Phase 17 — Public API

Phase 17 establishes:

- API versioning
- REST
- WebSocket
- authentication
- rate limiting
- OpenAPI
- webhooks
- streaming integrations
- SDK foundation
- monitoring

Again:

```text
Public API = access boundary
```

not:

```text
Public API = second runtime
```

---

# 11. Phase 18 is where we are now

The current roadmap says Phase 18 is:

# Production Hardening & Production Content

This is not a new architecture phase.

It is:

> **Make Phases 1–17 actually work together under production conditions.**

The current Phase 18 engineering track includes:

18.1 architecture/defect register  
18.2 regression remediation  
18.3 Ruff  
18.4 Mypy  
18.5 production API deployment  
18.6 live streaming providers  
18.7 webhooks  
18.8 observability  
18.9 load/soak  
18.10 Unity runtime  
18.11 live OBS  
18.12 unified config  
18.13 security  
18.14 disaster recovery  
18.15 migration rehearsal  
18.16 supply chain  
18.17 deployment architecture  
18.18 SLO/reliability  
18.19 tracing  
18.20 SDK productionisation  
18.21 API contract CI  
18.22 secrets/rotation  
18.23 external dependency resilience  
18.24 race-day rehearsal  
18.25 acceptance

Then:

18.26–18.53 = actual production content.

---

# 12. The biggest thing we have learned: the real F1 feeds

This is where the Baku investigations changed the project substantially.

We tested:

- FP1
- FP2
- FP3
- Qualifying
- Race

for the 2026 Baku weekend.

Meeting:

```text
1295
```

Sessions:

```text
11370 FP1
11371 FP2
11372 FP3
11373 Qualifying
11377 Race
```

And we tested **two fundamentally different classes of source**.

---

# 13. Official F1 live timing

We discovered that the official live timing system can provide much more useful telemetry when authenticated appropriately.

Without JWT:

```text
Timing
Tyres
Race control
Weather
Session state
etc.
```

But:

```text
Position.z = unavailable
CarData.z  = unavailable
```

That was observed in FP1 and FP2.

With authenticated access:

```text
Position.z
CarData.z
TimingData
TimingAppData
DriverRaceInfo
TrackStatus
WeatherData
etc.
```

became available.

That completely changes the preferred source architecture.

---

# 14. Official F1 is now the primary source

The current provider decision is:

| Role | Source |
|---|---|
| Primary live telemetry | Official F1 SignalR + authorised JWT |
| Fallback / supplement | OpenF1 MQTT |
| Timing authority | Official TimingData |
| Pose authority | Official Position.z |
| Car telemetry authority | Official CarData.z |
| Archive verification | Official static archive |

This is now much more evidence-based than the original “maybe OpenF1 is good enough” assumption.

---

# 15. Official `Position.z`

The official Position feed is compressed/base64 encoded and requires the dedicated decoder.

It provides:

- car identity
- source timestamp
- X
- Y
- Z
- status

The measured Qualifying characteristics were approximately:

```text
p50 sample interval ≈ 240 ms
p95 sample interval ≈ 420 ms
```

which works out to roughly:

```text
~4 Hz per car
```

This is enough for a digital twin if RaceVision handles the time model correctly.

---

# 16. Official `CarData.z`

We established the key channel mapping.

The observed channel mapping includes:

```text
0  = RPM
2  = Speed
3  = Gear
4  = Throttle
5  = Brake
45 = DRS when present
```

But DRS was **absent in the Baku validation captures**, so RaceVision must not assume DRS is always available.

That is extremely important.

The other major discovery:

```text
104 = unavailable/sentinel
```

So:

```text
throttle = 104
brake    = 104
```

does **not** mean 104%.

It means:

```text
unavailable
```

Likewise:

```text
0 RPM
```

may represent unavailable or stopped state depending on context.

RaceVision therefore needs explicit quality semantics rather than interpreting every number literally.

---

# 17. Position `(0,0,0)` is not necessarily a real position

We saw thousands of `(0,0,0)` samples during qualifying.

That means RaceVision must distinguish:

```text
real origin
```

from:

```text
position unavailable
```

For this project, the latter is the meaningful interpretation.

This also affects:

- Unity rendering
- stopped-car detection
- prediction
- interpolation
- incident detection

---

# 18. "OnTrack" doesn't mean "moving"

One of the most important findings:

Official Position status could remain:

```text
OnTrack
```

while a car was effectively stationary.

Therefore:

```text
Position.Status == OnTrack
```

cannot be used as:

```text
vehicle is moving
```

Instead RaceVision needs to combine:

- position displacement
- speed
- RPM
- TimingData stopped
- TimingData in-pit
- retired state
- DriverRaceInfo
- last known movement
- race control state

This is why I think the canonical vehicle state should eventually become something like:

```text
MOVING
STATIONARY
PIT
PIT_STATIONARY
FINISHED
RETIRED
HIDDEN
```

rather than simply relying on one feed flag.

---

# 19. Official TimingData is extremely important

We learned that TimingData is not merely “lap information”.

It is a primary state authority.

It contains delta-encoded state involving mechanisms such as:

```text
_deleted
_kf
```

which must be folded correctly.

After folding it, we were able to reproduce the final race classification.

Therefore:

> TimingData is not optional decoration; it is one of the main canonical race-state sources.

---

# 20. Race-specific discovery: DriverRaceInfo

During the Baku race, `DriverRaceInfo` turned out to be very valuable.

We observed:

- Gap
- Interval
- Catching
- OvertakeState
- IsOut
- PitStops
- Position

That gives RaceVision a much better basis for:

- battle analytics
- race overlays
- commentary
- overtaking context
- strategic context

than trying to infer everything from raw positions.

---

# 21. Pit stops

A major finding was that the official:

```text
PitStop
PitStopSeries
```

topics were silent during the race.

So we cannot build the project around assuming those topics will always exist.

Instead, pit stops can be reconstructed from:

```text
Official timing
+ InPit state
+ speed
+ zero-speed periods
+ PitLaneTimeCollection
+ CurrentTyres
+ OpenF1 pit
```

38 pit stops were reconstructed during the Baku race analysis.

The Official PitLaneTimeCollection duration agreed with OpenF1 where comparable.

This is a very strong pattern for RaceVision:

> **derive missing semantic events from authoritative lower-level state instead of depending on an unreliable optional topic.**

---

# 22. Overtakes

Likewise:

```text
Official OvertakeSeries
```

was silent.

But OpenF1 contained:

```text
160 overtakes
```

And position/timing changes can also be used to derive overtakes.

Therefore the architecture should be:

```text
Official position/timing = authoritative state
OpenF1 overtakes         = event enrichment/cross-check
derived logic            = resilient fallback
```

rather than:

```text
OvertakeSeries must exist
```

---

# 23. Intervals and gaps

Official `DriverRaceInfo` / TimingData provided:

- gap to leader
- interval ahead
- related race timing state

OpenF1 `intervals` provided corresponding information.

The values matched in validation examples.

Therefore this gives us another strong cross-provider equivalence check.

---

# 24. Starting grid

The strongest source identified was:

```text
Official TimingAppData
        ↓
GridPos
```

OpenF1's MQTT `starting_grid` did not provide the expected live race feed in the Baku test.

So:

```text
Official GridPos
```

is the preferred live authority.

---

# 25. Final classification

The race classification can be derived by correctly folding Official TimingData.

OpenF1's `session_result` was not the reliable live MQTT authority we initially might have expected.

So:

```text
Official TimingData
        ↓
final classification
```

is the correct design.

---

# 26. Safety Car and race state

The Baku race had:

```text
2 Safety Car windows
0 VSC
0 red flags
```

and these were obtainable from the official `TrackStatus`.

Race control messages were also cross-checked against OpenF1.

That confirms the model:

```text
Official TrackStatus / RCM
          ↓
authoritative race-state timeline
```

with OpenF1 as a cross-check/supplement.

---

# 27. OpenF1's actual role changed

OpenF1 is no longer viewed as:

> “The primary telemetry source.”

Instead it is:

> **A valuable secondary real-time source and supplementary event source.**

That is a big architectural improvement.

Current role:

```text
Official F1
   │
   ├── primary pose
   ├── primary car telemetry
   ├── primary timing
   ├── primary race state
   ├── primary tyres
   ├── primary weather
   └── primary session identity/state

OpenF1
   │
   ├── fallback live samples
   ├── supplementary events
   ├── pit information
   ├── overtakes
   ├── intervals
   └── cross-provider verification
```

---

# 28. OpenF1 MQTT is now particularly interesting

Current OpenF1 documentation confirms:

- OAuth2 authentication
- token endpoint
- 1-hour token expiry
- MQTT for backend live ingestion
- MQTT TLS on port `8883`
- MQTT-over-WebSockets on `wss://mqtt.openf1.org:8084/mqtt`
- token used as the MQTT password
- topic names mirror REST endpoints
- streaming messages include `_id` and `_key`

OpenF1 itself recommends MQTT for backend/non-browser real-time ingestion. citeturn127089search0

Its current account information also states authenticated users receive higher limits and up to 10 concurrent MQTT/WebSocket connections. citeturn127089search1

So the RaceVision backend architecture of:

```text
OpenF1 MQTT
      ↓
provider adapter
      ↓
canonical runtime
```

is well aligned with the documented OpenF1 interface.

---

# 29. The OpenF1 limitation we learned

OpenF1 live latency is **not constant**.

We measured approximately:

### FP3

```text
~4.6–4.7 s mid-session
~15 s backlog at end
```

### Qualifying

```text
p50 ~14.2–14.9 s
p95 ~24.5–25.6 s
```

### Race

```text
p50 ~3.15 s
p95 ~3.74 s
```

So this is critical:

> **Do not design RaceVision assuming OpenF1 always has a fixed 3-second delay.**

That was one of the most important empirical discoveries.

OpenF1 currently documents typical real-time updates around three seconds, but RaceVision's own Baku measurements demonstrate that backlog and disconnect behaviour can make the observed latency much higher. citeturn127089search2

---

# 30. Official latency

Official Position latency was much more stable.

Approximate observed:

### FP3

```text
~2.1–2.3 s
```

### Qualifying

```text
p50 ~2.17–2.23 s
p95 ~2.64–2.73 s
```

### Race

```text
p50 ~2.90 s
p95 ~3.42 s
```

That makes the Official feed much more appropriate as the primary real-time source for the RaceVision digital twin.

---

# 31. Official and OpenF1 data are remarkably equivalent

This was perhaps the strongest validation result.

### Qualifying

Approximately:

```text
179,973 location samples
175,566 car-data samples
```

matched the Official source at the exact source timestamp.

The comparison showed:

```text
position difference = 0.00 m
channel difference = 0
gear agreement     = 100%
```

### Race

Approximately:

```text
99.468% exact timestamp join
0.00 m spatial difference
```

That strongly indicates OpenF1 is republishing the same underlying sample stream rather than being a fundamentally different measurement system.

That means its main value to RaceVision is:

```text
availability
+
supplementary semantics
+
fallback
```

rather than:

```text
better physical precision
```

---

# 32. Official archive verification

This is equally important.

Qualifying:

```text
18,433 Position samples
17,837 CarData samples
```

were compared against the static archive.

They were:

```text
100% identical
```

Race:

```text
826,764 Position samples
791,692 CarData samples
```

were likewise:

```text
100% identical
```

So the architecture can safely use:

```text
LIVE
 ↓
canonical runtime
 ↓
PostgreSQL

ARCHIVE
 ↓
verification / gap backfill
 ↓
PostgreSQL
```

after the session.

That gives RaceVision a very useful post-race integrity mechanism.

---

# 33. What this means for prediction

Prediction should now be much narrower than it originally might have been.

The correct hierarchy is:

```text
1. Real Official telemetry
2. Matching real OpenF1 telemetry
3. Short bounded interpolation
4. Prediction
5. Hidden/unavailable
```

Not:

```text
Official → prediction automatically
```

The prediction system should only step in when actual samples are absent or insufficient.

This aligns exactly with the Phase 15 principle that prediction is derived.

---

# 34. What we learned about interpolation

During qualifying we measured roughly:

### Linear interpolation

```text
p50  ≈ 1.18 m
p95  ≈ 7.22 m
p99  ≈ 10.3 m
```

### Constant velocity extrapolation

```text
p50  ≈ 2.33 m
p95  ≈ 16.85 m
p99  ≈ 27.4 m
```

So interpolation between two real observations performed materially better than simply projecting forward.

That leads to a very clear rule:

> **Interpolate between real observations; predict only across genuine missing regions.**

---

# 35. Provider failover architecture

The evidence now supports a provider-health state machine.

Something like:

```text
HEALTHY
   ↓
DEGRADED
   ↓
FAILED
   ↓
RECOVERING
   ↓
HEALTHY
```

with hysteresis.

Why?

Because one late packet should not cause:

```text
Official → OpenF1
```

immediately.

Nor should one packet coming back immediately cause:

```text
OpenF1 → Official
```

again.

The source decision needs:

- gap threshold
- latency threshold
- disconnect state
- recovery hold time
- degradation state
- source provenance

The current README describes this as a target design rather than a completed canonical implementation.

That is correct.

---

# 36. Canonical VehicleState

The biggest architectural item emerging from the telemetry research is a future fused state.

Conceptually:

```text
VehicleState
├── identity
├── source_timestamp
├── position
├── orientation
├── speed
├── rpm
├── gear
├── throttle
├── brake
├── lap
├── sector
├── race_position
├── gap
├── interval
├── tyre
├── stint
├── pit_state
├── stopped_state
├── retirement_state
├── source_provenance
└── quality
```

And each major value needs provenance such as:

```text
OFFICIAL
OPENF1
ARCHIVE
PREDICTED
INTERPOLATED
UNAVAILABLE
```

That would be a very strong foundation for:

- Unity
- analytics
- battle detection
- commentary
- director
- replay
- API

But it remains a **target**, not something I would blindly bolt onto the runtime without respecting the existing ownership boundaries.

---

# 37. The data-quality model is now clear

RaceVision should explicitly understand:

```text
OBSERVED
INTERPOLATED
PREDICTED
UNAVAILABLE
```

rather than simply:

```text
float value
```

For example:

```text
speed = 0
```

could mean:

- car genuinely stopped
- sensor unavailable
- no fresh sample
- pit state
- decoder sentinel

Those are not equivalent.

This is why quality/provenance must eventually become first-class.

---

# 38. Storage lesson

Raw investigation data is large.

The Baku captures reached roughly gigabytes per session/weekend.

The key lesson is:

> Do not confuse forensic capture with the production runtime storage model.

The experimental Baku hub:

```text
logs/baku_weekend_web/
```

is not the production architecture.

It was useful for:

- raw capture
- debugging
- comparison
- latency analysis
- decoding investigation

But the production system should remain:

```text
Provider
 ↓
Adapter
 ↓
Canonical runtime
 ↓
selected persistence
```

rather than shipping the entire forensic hub into production.

---

# 39. Official topic coverage learned

The important Official feeds we have validated include:

```text
TimingData
TimingDataF1
TimingStats
TimingAppData
DriverTracker
LapSeries
TyreStintSeries
PitLaneTimeCollection
DriverList
CurrentTyres
TopThree
RaceControlMessages
TlaRcm
WeatherData
WeatherDataSeries
SessionData
TrackStatus
TeamRadio
SessionStatus
SessionInfo
ExtrapolatedClock
Heartbeat
ArchiveStatus
DriverRaceInfo
LapCount
ChampionshipPrediction
```

But not all are guaranteed to emit in every session.

And several were silent during the race:

```text
PitStop
PitStopSeries
OvertakeSeries
DriverScore
RcmSeries
SPFeed
```

This is why the architecture must use **semantic authority**, not merely a giant “subscribe to every topic and hope” model.

---

# 40. Important lesson about missing feeds

RaceVision should never assume:

```text
topic exists in schema
      =
topic emits useful data
```

The correct model is:

```text
Topic known
Topic configured
Topic observed
Topic currently active
Topic semantically usable
```

Those are different states.

This matters a lot for future production health monitoring.

---

# 41. Authentication

We established two different authentication domains.

### Official F1

Credentials/token remain entirely backend-side.

### OpenF1

Documented OAuth2 flow:

```text
username/password
      ↓
https://api.openf1.org/token
      ↓
access_token
      ↓
MQTT
```

Tokens expire after about one hour, so token refresh is part of a robust production implementation. citeturn127089search0

RaceVision should therefore never:

- embed tokens in Unity
- put provider secrets in frontend code
- commit token files
- print raw credentials
- expose MQTT credentials through the Public API

---

# 42. The biggest current architectural decision

At this point I would describe the live provider architecture as:

```text
                 ┌────────────────────┐
                 │  Official F1 JWT   │
                 └─────────┬──────────┘
                           ↓
                    Official SignalR
                           ↓
                 Position.z / CarData.z
                           │
                           │
                 TimingData / race state
                           │
                           ▼
                 ┌────────────────────┐
                 │ Canonical Runtime  │
                 └────────────────────┘
                           ▲
                           │
               fallback / supplementation
                           │
                     OpenF1 MQTT
                           │
                 location/car_data/etc.
```

And after the session:

```text
Official Archive
       ↓
verification
       ↓
backfill / integrity validation
```

That is now the core live-F1 strategy.

---

# 43. Where the repository stands right now

The current repository already contains substantial Official F1 infrastructure:

```text
backend/providers/official_f1/
```

including:

- catalogue
- authentication boundary
- Position decoder
- CarData decoder
- timing
- session
- race control
- tyres
- weather
- DriverRaceInfo
- topic registry
- archive support
- quality observer
- unknown capture
- runtime integration

And:

```text
backend/providers/openf1/
```

contains:

- provider adapter
- REST client
- streaming support
- dedupe
- token provider
- session filter
- connection state
- MQTT streaming client

So we are **not starting provider development from zero**.

We're integrating and hardening an already substantial provider layer.

---

# 44. One important inconsistency I found

The current README is now substantially updated and actually describes the Baku findings.

However, the older Phase 18 defect register still contains documentation defects such as:

```text
RV18-0200 README stale
RV18-0201 old roadmap inconsistency
```

Those entries appear to reflect the state at the 18.1 audit baseline rather than the current README.

Likewise, the README says work has landed for parts of the Official provider/archive hardening, while the main Roadmap still has some related Phase 18 checkboxes open.

So our next Cursor work should **reconcile documentation and actual implementation status**, rather than blindly adding duplicate systems.

---

# 45. What I think we should integrate first

I would **not** create a new Phase 19.

I would also **not** immediately rewrite the canonical runtime.

The next logical integration step is:

# Agent 18.6 — Live F1 Provider Production Integration & Validation

The purpose is to turn the research evidence into the production provider boundary.

That means:

```text
Official F1
    ↓
validated live provider

OpenF1
    ↓
validated fallback/supplement

Archive
    ↓
verified post-session source

All three
    ↓
existing canonical runtime
```

The future provider failover state machine and the full fused `VehicleState` should be introduced only where they fit the existing Phase 18 ownership model.

For the first integration, I would rather have Cursor **inspect the existing architecture and implement the smallest coherent production integration** than force a new architectural rewrite.

---

============================================================
3. HARD ARCHITECTURAL INVARIANTS
============================================================

These MUST remain true:

1. Phase 10 remains canonical runtime authority.
2. There is exactly one canonical runtime.
3. LIVE and REPLAY remain explicit modes of the same runtime.
4. Providers are adapters.
5. Provider payloads must not leak into Unity or other presentation layers.
6. Prediction is derived and never overwrites authoritative telemetry.
7. Interpolation is derived and never overwrites authoritative telemetry.
8. Public API is an adapter/access boundary.
9. Unity remains a presentation consumer.
10. OBS remains an output boundary.
11. Analytics remains derived.
12. Commentary remains derived.
13. Credentials remain backend-only.
14. No credentials or access tokens may be committed.
15. Production code must not depend on experimental Baku capture tooling.
16. Experimental forensic capture code must remain separate from production provider code.
17. Official F1 is the primary live telemetry source when authorised telemetry is available.
18. OpenF1 is a fallback/supplement, not the primary authority when Official telemetry is healthy.
19. Official static archive is a post-session verification/backfill source.
20. Do not silently invent missing telemetry values.

============================================================
4. VALIDATED BAKU 2026 EVIDENCE
============================================================

The integration must reflect these measured findings.

Meeting:

1295

Sessions:

11370 = FP1
11371 = FP2
11372 = FP3
11373 = Qualifying
11377 = Race

------------------------------------------------------------
OFFICIAL WITHOUT JWT
------------------------------------------------------------

FP1 and FP2 demonstrated:

- Timing/session/race-context feeds available.
- Position.z unavailable.
- CarData.z unavailable.

Therefore the implementation MUST NOT assume Position.z or CarData.z exists merely because an Official provider is connected.

------------------------------------------------------------
OFFICIAL WITH AUTHORISED JWT
------------------------------------------------------------

FP3:

- Position.z = 4,559 frames
- CarData.z = 4,551 frames

Qualifying:

- Position.z = 4,819 frames
- CarData.z = 4,777 frames

Race:

- Position.z available
- CarData.z available
- 479,314 CarData samples observed

Observed Official Position latency:

FP3:
approximately 2.1–2.3 s

Qualifying:
p50 approximately 2.17–2.23 s
p95 approximately 2.64–2.73 s

Race:
p50 approximately 2.90 s
p95 approximately 3.42 s

Use these only as documented measured evidence, not as permanent SLA guarantees.

------------------------------------------------------------
OFFICIAL POSITION CADENCE
------------------------------------------------------------

Qualifying observed:

- per-car p50 interval ≈ 240 ms
- p95 interval ≈ 420 ms
- approximately 3.8–4 Hz active-car rate
- 22 cars present in every Position frame
- no >2 s gaps during Q1–Q3 in the validated capture

Race frame interarrival was approximately 1 second because frames contained multi-car updates.

Do not incorrectly interpret frame interarrival as per-car telemetry frequency.

------------------------------------------------------------
OFFICIAL CARDATA CHANNELS
------------------------------------------------------------

Known observed channels:

0  = RPM
2  = Speed
3  = Gear
4  = Throttle
5  = Brake
45 = DRS when present

Important data-quality rules:

104 is an unavailable/sentinel value.

Therefore:

throttle == 104
brake == 104

must not be interpreted as real telemetry.

RPM == 0 must not automatically be treated as “engine actually at zero RPM” without context.

DRS channel 45 was absent in the Baku validation.

Do not assume DRS is mandatory.

Gear values must be validated.

Qualifying contained anomalous gear values > 8.

Race did not.

Speed > 380 km/h was not observed in the validation.

------------------------------------------------------------
POSITION QUALITY
------------------------------------------------------------

Position samples containing:

(0,0,0)

must be treated as an unavailable/no-fix condition unless the existing canonical model explicitly represents a valid origin with proven semantics.

Do not render these as a physical origin by default.

------------------------------------------------------------
POSITION STATUS
------------------------------------------------------------

Official Position status was not sufficient to determine vehicle motion.

Status could be:

OnTrack

while the car was stationary.

Therefore:

DO NOT implement:

status == OnTrack → moving

Instead use the existing runtime plus telemetry signals where available:

- speed
- displacement
- RPM
- TimingData stopped/in-pit/retired state
- DriverRaceInfo
- race control
- last moving sample

============================================================
5. OFFICIAL TIMING AUTHORITY
============================================================

Official TimingData remains the primary authority for:

- lap state
- sector state
- classification
- race timing
- timing deltas
- race position where appropriate

TimingData is delta/merge based and uses structures such as:

_deleted
_kf

The implementation must preserve correct delta folding semantics.

Do not replace TimingData authority with OpenF1.

============================================================
6. DRIVER RACE INFORMATION
============================================================

The Baku Race capture showed useful DriverRaceInfo fields including:

- Gap
- Interval
- Catching
- OvertakeState
- IsOut
- PitStops
- Position

Use these where the existing canonical event/timing architecture has an appropriate integration point.

Do not expose provider-specific DriverRaceInfo objects outside the provider/normalisation boundary.

============================================================
7. PIT STOP FINDINGS
============================================================

Baku Race demonstrated that Official:

PitStop
PitStopSeries

may be silent.

Therefore production behaviour MUST NOT depend on those topics existing.

Pit stops can be reconstructed using appropriate existing provider/runtime mechanisms from:

- TimingData
- InPit
- CarData speed
- stationary intervals
- PitLaneTimeCollection
- CurrentTyres
- OpenF1 pit where applicable

38 pit stops were reconstructed during the Baku Race investigation.

PitLaneTimeCollection duration agreed with OpenF1 pit duration where comparable.

Implement or harden only the integration point that belongs in this phase.

Do not create a second event system.

============================================================
8. OVERTAKE FINDINGS
============================================================

Official:

OvertakeSeries

was silent in the Baku Race.

OpenF1 contained:

160 overtakes

Therefore the production design must not depend on Official OvertakeSeries.

Use:

- Official position/timing changes as authoritative race-state signals.
- OpenF1 overtakes as supplementary event information.
- Existing analytics/event architecture for derived semantic overtakes.

Do not make OpenF1 the authority for official race classification.

============================================================
9. INTERVALS / GAPS
============================================================

DriverRaceInfo / TimingData

provided gap and interval information.

OpenF1:

intervals

matched values in validation examples.

Use Official as preferred authority and OpenF1 as fallback/supplement where appropriate.

============================================================

Official TimingAppData GridPos is the preferred live authority.

OpenF1 MQTT starting_grid was silent in the Baku Race validation.

11. FINAL CLASSIFICATION
============================================================

Final race classification must be derived from correctly folded Official TimingData.

OpenF1 session_result was not a reliable live MQTT dependency during the Baku Race validation.

============================================================

============================================================

Official TrackStatus is the primary authority for race-state/flag status.

Baku Race:

- 2 Safety Car windows
- 0 VSC
- 0 red flags

Race control signals were also cross-checked against OpenF1.

OpenF1 may supplement, but Official remains authority.

Current production OpenF1 streaming uses MQTT.

Keep all credentials backend-only.

Existing configuration must remain opt-in.

Expected production settings include the existing project configuration for:

OPENF1_USERNAME
OPENF1_PASSWORD
SESSION_EXECUTION_ENABLED

Use explicit topic subscriptions for production.

Use existing dedupe infrastructure.

14. OPENF1 LATENCY FINDINGS
============================================================

Measured Baku behaviour:

FP3:
approximately 4.6–4.7 s mid-session,
with approximately 15 s end-of-session backlog.

Qualifying:
p50 approximately 14.2–14.9 s
p95 approximately 24.5–25.6 s

Race:
p50 approximately 3.15 s
p95 approximately 3.74 s

Therefore:

DO NOT hardcode a universal OpenF1 latency.

Provider health logic must measure actual arrival/source timing.

Do not declare OpenF1 failed from a single late frame.

Do not declare it healthy again from a single new frame.

If provider health/failover already exists, harden it.

If provider failover is not yet owned by this agent, document the required integration boundary rather than creating a competing subsystem.

- 100% gear agreement

Race:

Approximately 99.468% exact timestamp join.

Spatial delta:

0.00 m

This indicates OpenF1 is extremely useful as a republished secondary source.

Use source timestamp for correlation.

receive_time == event_time
