```text
                    │   PostgreSQL   │
                    └────────────────┘
```

FastAPI Cloud deploys the FastAPI application in the cloud, and it supports environment variables/secrets for things such as `DATABASE_URL`. citeturn412663search0turn412663search1 FastAPI Cloud also provides autoscaling based on requests, so the number of running instances can increase with traffic. citeturn412663search2

Supabase provides a real PostgreSQL database, and its documentation specifically covers connecting persistent backends to the database using direct connections or pooled connections depending on the networking/runtime requirements. citeturn452938search0turn452938search1 FastAPI Cloud also has a documented Supabase integration using `DATABASE_URL`. citeturn412663search5

### So what does the Agent 18.9 result actually mean?

This:

```text
~85 requests/sec
1 worker
1 Windows PC
CPU saturated
```

**does not mean RaceVision's final FastAPI Cloud architecture is limited to 85 requests/sec.**

It means:

> In the particular local test environment, with one Python worker on your Ryzen laptop, the measured application throughput reached roughly that ceiling.

That is why the agent correctly called it:

```text
BASELINE_NOT_PRODUCTION_SLA
```

It is a **local engineering measurement**, not the capacity of your eventual FastAPI Cloud deployment.

### Your actual production topology changes the equation

Your intended environment is:

```text
User / Viewer / Unity / API clients
                │
                ▼
        FastAPI Cloud
        RaceVision API
                │
        ┌───────┴────────┐
        │                │
        ▼                ▼
   Supabase          external providers
   PostgreSQL        Official F1 / OpenF1
        │
        ▼
RaceVision persistent data
```

FastAPI Cloud can autoscale application instances based on request traffic. citeturn412663search2

So the next meaningful performance test is **not simply "can my laptop exceed 85 req/s?"**

It is:

```text
FastAPI Cloud
      +
Supabase
      +
actual production configuration
      +
multiple instances/autoscaling
      +
realistic workload
```

That is why I agree with Agent 18.9's recommendation to take the local result as a baseline and then validate on a staging-like environment.

---

# What about the database?

Your point about Supabase is especially important.

The Agent 18.9 report says:

```text
PostgreSQL isn't installed locally
```

That's **not actually a problem for your intended production architecture**.

You don't need PostgreSQL installed on your Windows machine just because RaceVision uses PostgreSQL.

Your intended setup is:

```text
Local development
    ↓
can use local/test database

FastAPI Cloud deployment
    ↓
DATABASE_URL
    ↓
Supabase PostgreSQL
```

Supabase explicitly provides PostgreSQL and connection methods for persistent backends and serverless environments. citeturn452938search0turn452938search3

And because FastAPI Cloud supports encrypted environment-variable secrets, your Supabase connection string should be configured there as a secret rather than committed to Git. citeturn412663search0turn412663search3

---

# There is one architectural issue we should pay attention to

This is actually more important than the 85 req/s measurement.

RaceVision is a **live F1 telemetry system**, not just a normal REST API.

FastAPI Cloud autoscaling means you can potentially have:

```text
Instance A
Instance B
Instance C
```

at the same time.

That raises the question:

> **Which instance owns the live F1 session and canonical telemetry runtime?**

You don't want this:

```text
Instance A → Official F1
Instance B → Official F1
Instance C → Official F1
```

all independently creating competing canonical runtimes.

Your architecture says there should be **one Session Runtime = canonical truth**.

So the performance/scaling architecture needs to preserve that invariant.

This is why I would not simply say "FastAPI Cloud autoscaling solves the 85 req/s issue."

FastAPI Cloud autoscaling solves **request-serving capacity**, but RaceVision's **live-session ownership model** has to remain deterministic across instances.

---

# And Supabase has a similar consideration

Multiple FastAPI Cloud instances can mean multiple database connections.

Supabase documents that application-side pooling and server-side poolers serve different purposes, and specifically recommends appropriate pooled connection modes when environments scale horizontally or create many short-lived connections. citeturn452938search1

For your eventual architecture, we therefore want:

```text
FastAPI Cloud instance A ─┐
FastAPI Cloud instance B ─┼──► Supabase connection strategy
FastAPI Cloud instance C ─┘
```

rather than allowing every instance to create uncontrolled PostgreSQL connections.

---

# So I would revise our interpretation of Agent 18.9

The result is actually useful because it gives us **two different engineering questions**:

### Local question — answered

```text
Can the RaceVision application handle
controlled concurrency and soak testing?

YES
```

with the measured local ceiling around 85 req/s.

### Production architecture question — still open

```text
How does RaceVision behave when deployed as:

FastAPI Cloud
+
Supabase PostgreSQL
+
multiple instances/autoscaling
+
live F1 provider
+
WebSockets
+
Prometheus/Grafana
?
```

That is a much more important benchmark.

---

# One correction I'd make to our Phase 18 thinking

I would **not make "increase worker count on the Windows PC" the next major RaceVision architecture task**.

Instead:

```text
18.9 local benchmark
        ✅
        │
        ▼
18.9 production/staging benchmark
        │
        ├── FastAPI Cloud
        ├── Supabase
        ├── realistic instance configuration
        ├── WebSockets
        ├── metrics
        └── live-session ownership
```

Then use the actual FastAPI Cloud measurements to decide whether CPU/instance sizing, autoscaling or application optimisation is necessary.

FastAPI Cloud itself says instance size and replica count solve different problems: instance size gives each replica more CPU/memory, while autoscaling adds/removes replicas based on traffic. citeturn412663search6

---

## Your final architecture should therefore look more like this

```text
                         RACEVISION

                         Viewers
                            │
             ┌──────────────┼──────────────┐
             │              │              │
          REST API       WebSocket       Future SDKs
             │              │
             └──────────────┼──────────────┘
                            ▼
                    ┌───────────────┐
                    │ FastAPI Cloud │
                    │ RaceVision    │
                    └───────┬───────┘
                            │
            ┌───────────────┼────────────────┐
            │               │                │
            ▼               ▼                ▼
       Supabase        Live Session      External F1
       PostgreSQL        Runtime          providers
            │               │
            │               ├── Official F1

            │

```

with:

```text
Unity

Prediction
Analytics
Commentary
```

remaining downstream consumers of the RaceVision runtime rather than separate competing sources of truth.

### Bottom line

**Yes, you remembered correctly.** The goal is **FastAPI Cloud for the backend + Supabase PostgreSQL for persistent data**. FastAPI Cloud can autoscale request-serving instances, and Supabase provides the managed PostgreSQL layer. citeturn412663search2turn452938search3

So the **85 req/s number from Agent 18.9 is not your production capacity**. It is a useful local baseline that exposed CPU/logging behaviour before we test the architecture where you actually intend to run it.

And that means I would keep **RV18-0607 open**, rather than treating it as "RaceVision can only do 85 req/s."

The next relevant work should validate the **FastAPI Cloud + Supabase deployment path under realistic load**, while separately ensuring that the single canonical live-session runtime remains correct when the cloud platform runs more than one application instance.

### What the results mean

If you get something like:


from `psql`, PostgreSQL is definitely installed.

If `pg_isready` reports:

```text
accepting connections
```

then PostgreSQL is actually running and ready.

If it reports:

```text
no response
```

then it is installed but the server isn't currently running.

## Assistant

Exactly — this is the important distinction.

**Prometheus and Grafana are not themselves the systems that connect to Official F1, OpenF1, or your RaceVision database.** In the current RaceVision design, they are primarily the **observability layer** watching what the RaceVision backend reports.

## The architecture

Your intended system is closer to this:

```text
                         ┌──────────────────────┐
                         │       USERS          │
                         │ Unity / Web / API    │
                         └──────────┬───────────┘
                                    │
                                    ▼
                         ┌──────────────────────┐
                         │    FASTAPI CLOUD     │
                         │      RACEVISION      │
                         │                      │
                         │  Session Runtime     │
                         │  LiveSessionAdapter  │
                         │  Public API          │
                         │  WebSockets          │
                         │  Monitoring          │
                         └───────┬───────┬──────┘
                                 │       │
              ┌──────────────────┘       └──────────────────┐
              │                                             │
              ▼                                             ▼
   ┌───────────────────┐                         ┌─────────────────────┐
   │  OFFICIAL F1      │                         │      OPENF1         │
   │  SignalR/WSS      │                         │     MQTT/WSS        │
   │                   │                         │                     │
   │ livetiming...     │                         │ mqtt.openf1.org    │
   │ F1TV JWT auth     │                         │ token/auth          │
   └─────────┬─────────┘                         └──────────┬──────────┘
             │                                             │
             └──────────────────┬──────────────────────────┘
                                ▼
                       ┌──────────────────┐
                       │  RaceVision      │
                       │  canonical live  │
                       │  runtime         │
                       └────────┬─────────┘
                                │
                                │ telemetry/state/health
                                ▼
                       ┌──────────────────┐
                       │ /api/v1/metrics  │
                       │ Prometheus text  │
                       └────────┬─────────┘
                                │
                         scrape every N sec
                                │
                                ▼
                       ┌──────────────────┐
                       │    PROMETHEUS    │
                       │ metrics storage  │
                       │ time series      │
                       │ alert rules      │
                       └────────┬─────────┘
                                │
                              query
                                │
                                ▼
                       ┌──────────────────┐
                       │     GRAFANA      │
                       │ dashboards       │
                       │ graphs            │
                       │ alerts/views      │
                       └──────────────────┘
```

Prometheus is the monitoring system that **scrapes metric HTTP endpoints and stores the resulting time series**. citeturn310862search10turn310862search8

Grafana is the **visualisation/query layer**. It connects to Prometheus as a data source and queries those stored metrics to build dashboards; it doesn't need to copy the underlying data into Grafana just to display it. citeturn310862search0turn310862search7

# So what does the current Agent 18.8 check actually check?

### 1. Supabase

**Not directly.**

Your current 18.8 setup checked whether **RaceVision's backend can see its configured database as healthy**.

So:

```text
RaceVision
   │
   │ database connection
   ▼
PostgreSQL
```

The monitoring exporter exposes things such as:

```text
racevision_database_up
racevision_database_migration_applied
racevision_database_check_latency_seconds
```

The manual test even deliberately simulated database failure and confirmed readiness went to 503 and the database-down alert fired.

But your current local test was **not connected to Supabase**.

For your final deployment:

```text
FastAPI Cloud
       │
       │ DATABASE_URL
       ▼
Supabase PostgreSQL
```

we need a later staging/production validation that proves that actual connection.

Supabase also exposes its own Prometheus-compatible Metrics API, including connection, CPU, I/O, WAL and query statistics. That is a **separate observability source** that we can potentially bring into the final monitoring architecture. citeturn310862search14turn310862search5

# 2. OpenF1

**The current 18.8 check does NOT prove a real OpenF1 live connection.**

It proves that RaceVision has the machinery to expose OpenF1 provider state, such as:

```text
connection state
reconnects
deduplication
malformed records
buffer depth
latency
token refresh metadata
```

The 18.8 report explicitly says real live Official F1/OpenF1 sessions were not run, and provider series only become live when a live session is actually running. fileciteturn103file0L94-L103 fileciteturn103file0L188-L195

So right now:

```text
OpenF1 code/observability       ✅ tested
OpenF1 real live connection     ❌ not validated
OpenF1 → telemetry → metrics    ❌ not validated live
```

Same answer.

The current system has the Official F1 provider and observability for:

```text
lifecycle
connection
authentication state
data quality
feed statistics
decode failures

out-of-order data
TimingData fold
```

but the manual validation **did not establish a real authenticated SignalR session against**:

```text
wss://livetiming.formula1.com/signalrcore
```

The report explicitly says that a real Official F1/OpenF1 session was not exercised. fileciteturn103file0L83-L92

So:

```text
Official F1 provider architecture    ✅
Official F1 monitoring metrics       ✅
F1 JWT secret protection             ✅
Real F1 JWT SignalR connection       ❌ not yet validated
Real F1 telemetry flowing            ❌ not yet validated in 18.8
```

And importantly, the JWT must remain **inside the FastAPI Cloud backend/provider boundary**. It should never be sent to Grafana or exposed as a Prometheus label.

---

# What Prometheus actually sees

Think of Prometheus as asking:

> **"RaceVision, how are you doing?"**

Every scrape is essentially:

```text
Prometheus
    │
    │ GET /api/v1/metrics
    ▼
RaceVision
    │
    ├── Database health
    ├── Transport health
    ├── API health
    ├── WebSocket stats
    ├── Official F1 provider stats
    ├── OpenF1 provider stats
    ├── Webhook stats
    └── Runtime/observability stats
```

Then Prometheus stores the answers.

The current Agent 18.8 exporter deliberately collects metrics from components that already own the state rather than creating a second runtime/source of truth. fileciteturn103file0L59-L70

---

# What Grafana sees

Grafana is one level further away:

```text
Official F1 ──┐
OpenF1 ───────┤
Supabase ─────┤
               ▼
           RaceVision
               │
               ▼
          Prometheus
               │
               ▼
            Grafana
```

Grafana is **not normally asking Official F1:**

> "Are you connected?"

Instead it asks Prometheus:

> "What did RaceVision report about Official F1 during the last hour?"

That distinction is fundamental.

Grafana can also connect directly to other data sources, including PostgreSQL and other monitoring systems. citeturn310862search0

---

# There are actually TWO levels of monitoring we eventually want

For your final RaceVision system, I'd distinguish them like this:

## Level 1 — RaceVision application observability

```text
                    RACEVISION
                        │
       ┌────────────────┼────────────────┐
       │                │                │
       ▼                ▼                ▼
   Official F1       OpenF1          Supabase
   provider          provider        database
       │                │                │
       └────────────────┼────────────────┘
                        ▼
                  provider/runtime
                     statistics
                        │
                        ▼
                  /api/v1/metrics
                        │
                        ▼
                   Prometheus
                        │
                        ▼
                    Grafana
```

This answers:

> Is RaceVision connected?  
> Is the provider healthy?  
> How many records arrived?  
> Are there duplicates?  
> Are connections/reconnects happening?  
> Is the database reachable?

That's what **18.8 is primarily building**.

---

## Level 2 — Infrastructure/provider monitoring

For production, we can go further:

```text
                    PROMETHEUS
                     ▲     ▲
                     │     │
       ┌─────────────┘     └──────────────┐
       │                                  │
       │                                  │
 FastAPI Cloud                         Supabase
 metrics                                Metrics API
       ▲                                  ▲
       │                                  │
       │                                  │
   RaceVision                         PostgreSQL
       │
       ├──────── Official F1
       │
       └──────── OpenF1
```

Supabase itself now provides a Prometheus-compatible Metrics API, so connection counts and database performance can be brought into the same observability ecosystem rather than relying only on RaceVision's own `database_up` health check. citeturn310862search14turn310862search2

That would allow Grafana eventually to show something like:

```text
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                 RACEVISION OPERATIONS                 │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ FASTAPI CLOUD                                         │
│ CPU      Memory      Requests     Errors              │
│                                                          │
│ DATABASE / SUPABASE                                    │
│ Connections    Pool usage    Query latency   DB CPU  │
│                                                          │
│ OFFICIAL F1                                            │
│ Connected     Auth OK     Feed quality    Last data  │
│                                                          │
│ OPENF1                                                │
│ Connected     Reconnects  Latency         Buffer     │
│                                                          │
│ LIVE SESSION                                          │
│ Cars     Frames/s     Session     Provider           │
│                                                          │
│ PROMETHEUS                                            │
│ Scrape UP     Errors     Metric health               │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

That's much closer to what I think you ultimately want.

---

# So did Agent 18.8 prove the full live chain?

**No.**

It proved the **monitoring plumbing locally**:

```text
RaceVision
    ↓
/metrics
    ↓
Prometheus 3.15
    ↓
Grafana 13.2
```

and it proved simulated/controlled provider and database states can be represented.

It did **not** prove this yet:

```text
FastAPI Cloud
     ↓
Supabase
     ↓
Official F1 SignalR + JWT
     ↓
OpenF1 MQTT/WSS
     ↓
LiveSessionAdapter
     ↓
Canonical telemetry
     ↓
Prometheus
     ↓
Grafana
```

The Agent 18.8 report explicitly keeps the relevant live-provider/staging work open for exactly this reason. fileciteturn103file0L154-L169

### And this is actually good architecture

We **don't want Prometheus or Grafana connecting directly to the F1 feeds**.

The desired ownership should be:

```text
Official F1 ───────► RaceVision
OpenF1 ────────────► RaceVision
Supabase ──────────► RaceVision

RaceVision ────────► Prometheus
Prometheus ────────► Grafana
```

That keeps **RaceVision as the canonical runtime**, rather than turning Grafana/Prometheus into another telemetry consumer or another source of truth.

For Supabase specifically, we can additionally scrape Supabase's own Metrics API into the monitoring stack later to get database-level connection/pooling information. Supabase documents that live connection inspection can also be performed through PostgreSQL's `pg_stat_activity`. citeturn310862search2turn310862search14

So your instinct is right: **the current 18.8 check is only the monitoring foundation. The really important future validation is the complete FastAPI Cloud + Supabase + Official F1 + OpenF1 live chain.**
